\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\sum_{j=3}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6,1)}\right].

Similarly,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+2,n]})\right],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}\otimes X_{j+1}\otimes(X_{j}+X_{j+1})]\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\sum_{j=3}^{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6,2)}\right],\enskip\text{and}\enskip

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-2}\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+3,n]})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{j}\otimes X_{j+1}\otimes X_{j+2}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\sum_{j=3}^{n-2}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6,3)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6,3)}\right],

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,2)}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,3)}
are similarly derived sixth-order remainder terms.
Putting all above terms together, we get

\displaystyle\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\phi\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=3}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert,

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6)}=\frac{1}{6}\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,1)}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,2)}+\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,3)}
.
A similar remainder lemma with
Lemma

4.3
can be given for
\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(4,1)}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6)}
(see
Lemma

A.1
), and summing up the upperbounds iteratively over
k
and
j
results in a finite fourth moment version of
Lemma

4.4
(see
Lemma

A.2
). Finally, the dual induction of this lemma with
Lemma

4.5
proves the desired Berry–Esseen bound with finite fourth moments. The proof details are relegated to
Section

A.1
.

Remark 4.6
.

As we discussed in
Section

2.3.1
, the bottleneck of our Berry–Esseen bound is often the first term with the third moment (i.e.,
L_{3}
). A significant improvement by the iterated Lindeberg swapping is reducing the term’s order of
\log(ep)
from
\sqrt{\log(pn)}\log^{2}(ep)
to
\sqrt{\log(pn)}\log^{3/2}(ep)
. One may repeat the Lindeberg swapping to further improve the order. For example, the first term of
\mathfrak{R}^{(6)}_{X_{j},W_{k}}
is

\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{2}\left\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k)}+tW_{k}+Y_{(k,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle~dt.

Based on the Taylor expansion up to order
7
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{2}\left\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k)}+tW_{k}+Y_{(k,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle~dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k)}+Y_{(k,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{3}\left\langle\nabla^{7}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k)}+tW_{k}+Y_{(k,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle~dt

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k-1)}+Y_{(k+1,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\int_{0}^{1}\Big\langle\nabla^{7}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k-1)}+t(X_{k-1}+Y_{k+1})+Y_{(k+1,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),

\displaystyle X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\otimes(X_{k-1}+Y_{k+1})\Big\rangle~dt

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{6}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{3}\left\langle\nabla^{7}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k)}+tW_{k}+Y_{(k,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 4}\right\rangle~dt.

Like
Eq.

26
, one may decompose the first term and re-apply the Lindeberg swapping:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,k-1)}+Y_{(k+1,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(Y_{[1,k-1)}+Y_{(k+1,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\sum_{l=1}^{k-2}\frac{1}{6}\Big\langle\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,l)}+X_{l}+Y_{(l,k-1)}+Y_{(k+1,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)})

\displaystyle-\nabla^{6}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,l)}+Y_{l}+Y_{(l,k-1)}+Y_{(k+1,j-1)\cup(j+1,n)}),X_{j}^{\otimes 3}\otimes W_{k}^{\otimes 3}\Big\rangle.

To make a successful improvement, we recommend using piece-wise quadratic
f_{r,\phi}
, instead of the piece-wise linear one defined in
Section

4.1
. This choice of
f_{r,\phi}
allows improved remainder lemmas for the sixth, seventh and ninth-order remainder terms. At the end, infinitely repeating the Lindeberg swapping may improve the order asymptotically to
\sqrt{\log(pn)}\log(ep)
.

In this paper, we do not pursue further refining
Theorem

2.2
. Because
Y_{j}
is Gaussian, the dimension complexity cannot be improved from
\log^{4}(ep)
due to the last term with the
q
-th moment (i.e.,
\nu_{q}
). Hence, further Lindeberg swappings do not help match the dimension complexity of
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov and
Koike 2023
under bounded
X_{j}
.

4.5
Without Assumption (
VAR-EV
)

Assumption (
VAR-EV
) was invoked at specific points in the proofs for the simplified versions. We made use of this assumption in two key instances: first, in our selection of
J_{n}
during the "Partitioning the sum" step in
Section

4.1
, and second, in obtaining the upper bound in
Eq.

27
.

For the first case, without (
VAR-EV
), let
J_{n}\equiv n\left(1-\min\left\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\log^{2}(4ep)}\right\}\right)
, which guarantees
J_{n}\geq\frac{n}{2}
naturally. Then, the rightmost upper bound in
Eq.

16
is
\frac{C}{\underline{\sigma}^{3}\sqrt{n}}
rather than
\frac{C\log(ep)}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\sqrt{n}}
. Summing the replaced upper bound over
j
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\min\{\sigma_{\min},\underline{\sigma}\sqrt{\log(ep)}\}}\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)\left[{L}_{3,\max}+\phi^{q-3}\nu_{q,\max}\right]

\displaystyle+C\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\left[{L}_{3,\max}+\phi^{q-3}\nu_{q,\max}\right]\kappa_{[1,j-3)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

The resulting induction lemma from
\kappa
to
\mu
is (that is,
Lemma

4.4
becomes)

\displaystyle\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\phi\sigma_{\min}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\min\{\sigma_{\min},\underline{\sigma}\sqrt{\log(ep)}\}}\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)\left[{L}_{3,\max}+\phi^{q-3}\nu_{q,\max}\right]

\displaystyle+C\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\left[{L}_{3,\max}+\phi^{q-3}\nu_{q,\max}\right]\kappa_{[1,j-3)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o}),

and the same dual induction derives the desired conclusion.

For
Eq.

27
, without (
VAR-EV
), the rightmost upper bound is
\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}L_{3,j}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{3}}
rather than
\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}L_{3,j}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}
. That is,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(Y_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+1,n)})\right],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{\otimes 3}]\right\rangle\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}L_{3,j}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{3}}.

As a result,

\displaystyle\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\phi\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\min\{\sigma_{\min},\underline{\sigma}\sqrt{\log(ep)}\}}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=3}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert.

Moreover, the summation of
\mathfrak{R}^{(4,1)}_{X_{j}}
and
\mathfrak{R}^{(6)}_{X_{j},X_{k}}
, we use the same
J_{n}
to partition them. The dual induction based on the resulting induction lemma derives the desired conclusion.

4.6
1
-ring dependence and permutation argument

We note that the Lindeberg swapping in
Eq.

13
is not symmetric with respect to the indices. The asymmetry resulted in a worse rates in
Lemmas

4.4
and
4.5
, by having the maximal moment terms,
L_{3,\max}
and
\nu_{q,\max}
. To obtain an improved Berry–Esseen bounds with averaged moment terms,
\overline{L}_{3}
and
\overline{\nu}_{q}
, as in
Theorems

2.1
and
2.2
, it is desired to relax the asymmetry in the Lindeberg swappings. One such way is to take the average of the upper bounds over permutations of the indices as done in
Deng 2020
;
Deng and Zhang 2020
. However, because
1
-dependence is specific to the index ordering, the only permutation preserving the dependence structure is the flipping of the indices (i.e.,
X_{1}\mapsto X_{n},X_{2}\mapsto X_{n-1},\dots,X_{n}\mapsto X_{1}
), which is not sufficient for our purpose. We allow more permutations by weakening the dependence structure to
1
-ring dependence. By allowing
X_{1}
and
X_{n}
dependent on each other, index rotations (i.e.,
X_{1}\mapsto X_{j_{o}},X_{2}\mapsto X_{j_{o}+1},\dots,X_{n}\mapsto X_{j_{o}-1}
with some
j_{o}\in[1,n]
) are added to the catalog of available permutations. By averaging the upper bound in
Eq.

13
over the permutations, we obtain

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

(30)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)_{n}}+X_{[j_{o}+j]_{n}}+Y_{(j_{o}+j,j_{o}+n]_{n}})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)_{n}}+Y_{[j_{o}+j]_{n}}+Y_{(j_{o}+j,j_{o}+n]_{n}})\right]\right|,

where
[j_{o}+j]_{n}
is
j_{o}+j
modulo
n
, and

[i,j]_{n}\equiv\{[i]_{n},[i+1]_{n},\dots,[j-1]_{n},[j]_{n}\}.

The subscript
n
notates that the interval is defined modulo
n
. If the ambient modulo is obvious, we omit the subscript. The other types of intervals,
(i,j]_{n},[i,j)_{n}
and
(i,j)_{n}
, are similarly defined. For full notation details, please refer to
Section

A.2
. A similar permutation argument also applies to
Lemma

4.5
; see
Lemma

A.6
. The dual induction on the resulting induction lemmas proves
Theorem

2.1
for
3\leq q<4
. We relegate the proof details to
Section

A.3
.

For
4\leq q
, there is the second Lindeberg swapping during the decomposition of the third order remainder terms (e.g.,
Eq.

28
). The same permutation argument as in
Eq.

30
provides the following averaged version: for
3\leq j\leq n
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+1,n]})-\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+1,n]})],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{\otimes 3}]\right\rangle

(31)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\Big\langle\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-2}}+X_{[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}+Y_{(k_{o}+k,k_{o}+j-1)_{j-2}\cup(j,n]})

\displaystyle-\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-2}}+Y_{[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}+Y_{(k_{o}+k,k_{o}+j-1)_{j-2}\cup(j,n]})],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{\otimes 3}]\Big\rangle.

Then, the dual induction with
Lemma

A.6
proves
Theorem

2.2
for
4\leq q
. We relegate the proof details to
Section

A.4
.

A
Proof of Theorems

A.1
Proof details of Section
4.4

We recall from
Section

4.4
that

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert=\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=3}^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert,

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6)}=\frac{1}{6}\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,1)}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,2)}+\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6,3)}
.The upper bounds of the remainder terms are given as the following lemma.

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose that Assumption (
MIN-EV
) holds. For
W
representing either
X
or
Y
and
j,k\in[1,n]
such that
k\leq j-2
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\left[{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{1,\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\},

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi L_{3,\max}\left[\begin{aligned} L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q-1}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{1,\kappa_{[1,k-3)}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k}\}.

where
\delta_{n-j}^{2}\equiv\delta^{2}+\underline{\sigma}^{2}\max\{n-j,0\}
,
\delta^{o}_{n-j}\equiv 12\delta_{n-j}\sqrt{\log(pn)}
and
\kappa^{o}_{j}\equiv\frac{\delta_{n-j}\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}\sqrt{\max\{j,1\}}}
, as long as
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
and
\phi\delta\geq\frac{1}{\log(ep)}
.

Back to
Eq.

13
, we get

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}

\displaystyle+C\phi\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]\min\{1,\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle+C\phi\sum_{j=3}^{n}L_{3,\max}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{1,\kappa_{[1,k-3)]}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k}\},

Partitioning the sum.

Again, we partition the summations at
J_{n}=n(1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\log^{2}(4ep)})
. For the first summation, similar calculations with the finite third moment cases lead to

\displaystyle C\phi\sum_{j=1}^{n}\left[{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(0,j)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\left[{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+1)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-4}}\right]\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle+C\phi\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\left[{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]\kappa_{[1,j)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

by noting
Eq.

16
and that

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}\frac{1}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}\frac{1}{\delta^{q-4}\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\leq\frac{C\log(ep)}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-4}\sqrt{n}},

(32)

\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{\kappa^{o}_{j-1}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\leq\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{\log(ep)}{\delta^{q-4}\delta_{n-j}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\sqrt{j-1}}\leq\frac{C\log(ep)}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-4}\sqrt{n}}\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right),

(33)

for some universal constant
C>0
. On the other hand, for the second summation,

\displaystyle C\phi\sum_{j=3}^{n}{L}_{3,\max}\sum_{k=1}^{(j-2)\wedge\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=3}^{n}{L}_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}^{2}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}{L}_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-3}}\right],

where the last inequality comes from
\sum_{j=3}^{n}\frac{1}{\delta^{2}_{n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}}\leq\sum_{j=3}^{n}\frac{1}{(n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor)\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}
, and

\displaystyle C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n-1}L_{3,\max}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[L_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]\kappa^{o}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n-1}L_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-j}^{2}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}{L}_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-3}}\right]\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right),

where the inequliaties follow
Eqs.

33
and
23
, respectively.
In sum, we obtain a finite fourth moment version of
Lemma

4.4
:

Lemma A.2
.

If Assumptions (
MIN-VAR
), (
MIN-EV
) and (
VAR-EV
) hold, and
q\geq 4
, then
for any
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\phi\sigma_{\min}}+{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\left[{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+1)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-4}}\right]\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}{L}_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-3}}\right]\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)

\displaystyle+C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\left[L_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle+C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}L_{3,\max}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[L_{3\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]\kappa_{[1,k-3)}(\delta_{n-k}^{o}),

for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Dual Induction.

In this case, our induction hypothesis on
n
is

\sqrt{n}\mu_{[1,n]}\leq\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n}\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)},

(HYP-BE-2)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{1}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{2}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{3}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2/(q-2)}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

for universal constants
\mathfrak{C}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{3}
whose values do not change in this subsection.
If
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2},\mathfrak{C}_{3}\geq 1
, then (
HYP-BE-2
), requiring
\mu_{[1,n]}\leq 1
only, trivially holds for
n\leq 9
.

Now we consider the case of
n>9
. A similar induction argument with the finite third moment cases proves

\sqrt{i}\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)\leq\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i}{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i}{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i}\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,i}\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}\delta,

(HYP-AC-2)

where
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{1,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{2,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{3,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pi)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
and

\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}=\mathfrak{C}_{5,\kappa}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
for some universal constants
\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{5,\kappa}
whose values do not change in this subsection.

Now we prove (
HYP-BE-2
) on
n
. We first upper bound the last two terms in
Lemma

A.2
:

\displaystyle C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\left[L_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle+C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}L_{3,\max}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[L_{3\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]\kappa_{[1,k-3)}(\delta_{n-k}^{o}).

Applying (
HYP-AC-2
) to
\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})
,

\displaystyle C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\left[L_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\left[L_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,j-5}~{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,j-5}~{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,j-5}~\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,j-5}~\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}\delta_{n-j}^{o}\\
\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\left[L_{4,\max}(\log(ep))^{3/2}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta^{q-4}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &(\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,j-5}~{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,j-5}~{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,j-5}~\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,j-5}~\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2})\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{1}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\\
&+\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}\sqrt{\log(pn)}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{1}{\delta_{n-j}^{2}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\left[L_{4,\max}(\log(ep))^{3/2}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta^{q-4}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &(\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n-1}~{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n-1}~{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n-1}~\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)})\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\frac{\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}}{\delta}\\
&+\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)\log(pn)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)\end{aligned}\right].

by
Eq.

23
and that
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,n-1}=\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n-1}
,
\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,n-1}=\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n-1}
,
\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,n-1}=\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n-1}
,
\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,n-1}=\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(p(n-1))}}{\sigma_{\min}}
and
\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}=\mathfrak{C}_{5,\kappa}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
.
Similarly,

\displaystyle C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}L_{3,\max}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[L_{3\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]\kappa_{[1,k-3)}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}L_{3,\max}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[L_{3\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,k-4}~{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,k-4}~{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,k-4}~\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,k-4}~\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}\delta_{n-j}^{o}\\
\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}L_{3,\max}\left[L_{3\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,k-4}~{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,k-4}~{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,k-4}~\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,k-4}~\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}\delta_{n-j}^{o}\\
\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\phi L_{3,\max}\left[L_{3\max}(\log(ep))^{5/2}+\nu_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta^{q-3}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &(\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n-1}~{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n-1}~{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n-1}~\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)})\frac{1}{\delta}+\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\frac{\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}}{\delta}\\
&+\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)\log(pn)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)\end{aligned}\right].

In sum, as long as
\delta\geq\nu_{2,\max}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log(ep)}\geq\sigma_{\min}
and
\phi>0
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathfrak{C}^{(4)}\phi\left[\begin{aligned} &{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\delta}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q-1)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\delta^{q-3}}\\
&+{L}_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\delta}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\delta^{q-2}}\right]\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} \widetilde{\mu}_{1,n-1}{L}_{3,\max}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n-1}{L}_{4,\max}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n-1}\nu_{q,\max}^{1/(q-2)}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{C}^{(4)}\left[\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\phi\sigma_{\min}}+{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{C}^{(4)}\phi\left[\begin{aligned} &{L}_{4,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q-1)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\delta^{q-4}}\\
&+{L}_{3,\max}\left[{L}_{3,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}}+{\nu}_{q,\max}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\delta^{q-3}}\right]\end{aligned}\right]\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)

where
\mathfrak{C}^{(4)}
is a universal constant whose value does not change in this subsection.
Taking
\delta=8\mathfrak{C}^{(4)}\left(\frac{{L}_{3,\max}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\sqrt{\log(ep)}+\left(\frac{{L}_{4,\max}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+\left(\frac{\nu_{q,\max}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\right)^{\frac{1}{q-2}}\right)\sqrt{\log(ep)}\geq\overline{\nu}_{2}^{1/2}\sqrt{\log(ep)}
and
\phi=\frac{1}{\delta\sqrt{\log(ep)}}
,

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\max_{j<n}\widetilde{\mu}_{1,j}\overline{L}_{3}+\frac{1}{2}\max_{j<n}\widetilde{\mu}_{2,j}\overline{L}_{4}^{1/2}+\frac{1}{2}\max_{j<n}\widetilde{\mu}_{3,j}\overline{\nu}_{q}^{1/(q-2)}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{C}^{(5)}\left(\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}+\overline{L}_{4}^{1/2}\frac{\log(ep)}{\underline{\sigma}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}^{1/(q-2)}\frac{\log(ep)}{\underline{\sigma}^{2/(q-2)}}\right)\frac{\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

for another universal constant
\mathfrak{C}^{(5)}
, whose value only depends on
\mathfrak{C}^{\prime\prime}
.
Taking
\mathfrak{C}_{1}=\mathfrak{C}_{2}=\mathfrak{C}_{3}=\max\{2\mathfrak{C}^{(5)},1\}
,

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{1}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{2}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{3}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2/(q-2)}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

satisfies

\displaystyle\sqrt{n}\mu_{[1,n]}\leq\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}\overline{L}_{3}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}\overline{L}_{4}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n}\overline{\nu}_{q}^{1/(q-2)},

which proves (
HYP-BE-2
) at
n
.
This proves our theorem.

A.2
Review on the ring
\mathbb{Z}_{n}

To facilitate the notations under permutation arguments, we introduce the notion of integer ring
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
. Let
\mathbb{Z}_{n}\equiv\mathbb{Z}/n\mathbb{Z}
be the ring with additive operation
+
and multiplicative operation
\cdot
of modulo
n
. For brevity, we allow a slight notational conflict to denote the elements by
1,\dots,n
such that
n(=0)
and
1
are additive and multiplicative identities, respectively. It means that the next element of
n
is
1
, which is the same as
n+1
modulo
n
. When we need to specify, we denote
i
modulo
n
by
[i]_{n}
. We reference textbooks in abstract algebra such as
Lang 2012
for detailed properties of the ring structure.

We also define a distance and intervals in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
. For
i,j\in\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, the distance between
i
and
j
is defined as

d(i,j)=\min\{\lvert i-j+kn\rvert:k\in\mathbb{Z}\},

where the operations inside the parentheses are on
\mathbb{Z}
, and the closed interval is defined as

[i,j]_{n}=\{[i]_{n},[i+1]_{n},\dots,[j-1]_{n},[j]_{n}\}.

The subscript
n
notates that the interval is defined over the ring
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
. If the ambient ring is obvious, we omit the subscript.
The other types of intervals,
(i,j]_{n},[i,j)_{n}
and
(i,j)_{n}
, are defined similarly.

A.3
Proof of Theorem
2.1

The proof for
1
-ring dependent cases with finite third moments is similar with the
1
-dependent cases in
Section

4
. In
1
-ring depent cases, we need to address the additional dependence between
X_{1}
and
X_{n}
and the average across the permutations in
Section

4.6
.

Breaking the ring.
First, we note that the Berry-Esseen bound under
1
-ring dependence can be reduced to the bound under
1
-dependence:

\displaystyle\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle+\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})]\right\rvert+\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})]\right\rvert.

Note that we removed
X_{n}
and
Y_{n}
from
X_{[1,n]}
and
Y_{[1,n]}
, respectively, to break the
1
-ring dependence. By the Taylor expansion centered at
X_{[1,n)}
,

\displaystyle\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})

(34)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-1)}),X_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(2,n-1)}),X_{n}\otimes X_{1}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-2)}),X_{n-1}\otimes X_{n}\right\rangle+\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)},

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)}
is specified in
Section

C
. This is the same for
\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})
but with
Y
in place of
X
.

First Lindeberg swapping.
We bound
\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})]\right\rvert
by the Lindeberg swapping as in
Section

4.1
. Here we define

W_{[i,j]}^{\mathsf{C}}\equiv X_{[1,i)}+Y_{(j,n)}.

Note that unlike
Section

4.1
, the
n
-th element is removed. Then,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(W^{\mathsf{C}}_{[j,j]}+X_{j})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(W^{\mathsf{C}}_{[j,j]}+Y_{j})\right]=\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right],

(35)

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}
are remainder terms of the Taylor expansions specified in
Section

C.2
.

Second moment decomposition and second Lindeberg swapping.

To bound the second moment terms in
Eq.

34
, we re-apply the Lindeberg swapping. For simplicity, we only look at
\left\langle\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-1)}),X_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right\rangle
, but similar arguments work for the other second moment terms. Because
\mathbb{E}[X_{n}^{\otimes 2}]=\mathbb{E}[Y_{n}^{\otimes 2}]
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right]\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{(1,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[Y_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,j)}+X_{j}+Y_{(j,n-1)})-\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,j)}+Y_{j}+Y_{(j,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right]\right\rangle,

By the Taylor expansion up to order
3
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right]\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{(1,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}^{\otimes 2}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2,1)}\right],

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2,1)}
is the third-order remainder, specified in
Section

C.3
. Similarly,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(2,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}\otimes X_{1}\right]\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{(2,n-1)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}\otimes X_{1}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=3}^{n-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2,2)}\right],\enskip\text{and}\enskip

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-2)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}\otimes X_{n-1}\right]\right\rangle-\left\langle\mathbb{E}\left[\nabla^{2}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{(1,n-2)})\right],\mathbb{E}\left[X_{n}\otimes X_{n-1}\right]\right\rangle

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=2}^{n-3}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2,3)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2,3)}\right],

where
\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2,2)}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2,3)}
are simlarly derived third-order remainder terms.
Putting all the above terms together,

\displaystyle\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})+\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y}^{(3)}+\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2)}\right],

where
\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2)}=\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2,1)}+\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2,2)}+\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2,3)}
, and

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})+\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y}^{(3)}\right\rvert+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2)}\right]\right\rvert.

Remainder lemma.
Similar to
Section

4.1
, the remainder terms
\mathfrak{R}_{W}^{(3)}
,
\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,1)}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2)}
are upper bounded by conditional anti-concentration probability bounds. For
q>0
, let

\widetilde{L}_{q,j}\equiv\sum_{j^{\prime}=j-3}^{j+3}L_{q,j^{\prime}}\enskip\text{and}\enskip\widetilde{L}_{q,[k]_{j-2}}\equiv\sum_{k^{\prime}=k-3}^{k+3}L_{q,[k^{\prime}]_{j-2}},

where
[k^{\prime}]_{j-2}
is
k^{\prime}
modulo
j-2
, and
\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j}
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{q,[k]_{j-2}}
are similarly defined.

Lemma A.3
.

Suppose that Assumption (
MIN-EV
) holds. For
W
representing either
X
or
Y
and
j\in\mathbb{Z}_{n}
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W}^{(3)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,n}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,n}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta^{3}}\min\{\kappa_{(2,n-1)}(\delta_{0}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{n},1\},

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\min\{\kappa_{[1,j-3)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j},1\},

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(3,2)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\begin{aligned} \widetilde{L}_{3,j}+\widetilde{L}_{3,n}+\phi^{q-3}\left(\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j}+\nu_{q,n}\right)\end{aligned}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{(1,j-2)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j},1\},

where
\delta_{n-j}^{2}\equiv\delta^{2}+\underline{\sigma}^{2}\max\{n-j,0\}
,
\delta^{o}_{n-j}\equiv 12\delta_{n-j}\sqrt{\log(pn)}
and
\kappa^{o}_{j}\equiv\frac{\delta_{n-j}\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}\sqrt{\max\{j,1\}}}
, as long as
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
and
\phi\delta\geq\frac{1}{\log(ep)}
.

Permutation argument.

We apply the permutation arguement to
Eq.

13
as in
Eq.

30
.

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left|\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}+X_{j_{o}+j}+Y_{(j_{o}+j,j_{o}+n]})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}+Y_{j_{o}+j}+Y_{(j_{o}+j,j_{o}+n]})\right]\right|,

where the indices of
X
and
Y
are defined in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
.
Together with the results in
Lemma

A.3
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta^{3}}\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(2,n-1)}(\delta_{0}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{n}\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-2)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-3)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\},

where
j_{o}+(2,n-1)
is the shifted interval of
(2,n-1)
by
j_{o}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, namely,
\{[j_{o}+3]_{n},\dots,[j_{o}+n-2]_{n}\}
.

Partitioning the sum.

We partition the summation over
j
at

J_{n}=n(1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\log^{2}(4ep)})
. A notable distinction from
Section

4.1
is that we should also take averages over
j_{o}
alongside the summations over
j
.
For
j<J_{n}
,

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j<J_{n}}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-3)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j<J_{n}}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}

\displaystyle=C\sum_{j<J_{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{3}+\phi^{q-3}\overline{\nu}_{q}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{3}+\phi^{q-3}\overline{\nu}_{q}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}},

because of
Eq.

16
.
For
j\geq J_{n}
,

\displaystyle\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(0,+j)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\kappa_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle+C\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{3}+\phi^{q-3}\overline{\nu}_{q}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\kappa^{o}_{j-1}.

The last term is upper bounded by

\displaystyle C\sum_{j\geq J_{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{3}+\phi^{q-3}\overline{\nu}_{q}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\kappa^{o}_{j-1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{3}+\phi^{q-3}\overline{\nu}_{q}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right)

because of
Eq.

17
.
In sum, we obtain the following induction from
\kappa_{I}(\delta)
for
I\subset\mathbb{Z}_{n}
to

\mu_{[1,n]}=\mu(X_{[1,n]},Y_{[1,n]}).

Lemma A.4
.

If Assumptions (
MIN-VAR
), (
MIN-EV
) and (
VAR-EV
) hold, then
for any
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\delta\log(ep)+\sqrt{\log(ep)}/\phi}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{3}+\phi^{q-3}\overline{\nu}_{q}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\log(en)

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\kappa_{j_{o}+(3,j-2)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n-1}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}+\phi^{q-3}\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\right]\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o}),

for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Anti-concentration inequality.

We note the following monotonic property of
\kappa
:

Lemma A.5
.

Suppose that
I_{0}
and
I
are intervals in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
satisfying
I^{\prime}\subset I
and that
\delta^{\prime}\geq\delta>0
. Then,
\kappa_{I}(\delta)\leq\kappa_{I^{\prime}}(\delta^{\prime})
.

\displaystyle\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)

\displaystyle=\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbb{P}[{X}_{I}\in A_{r,\delta}|\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{Z}_{n}\setminus I}]

\displaystyle=\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}[{X}_{I^{\prime}}\in A_{r-X_{I\setminus I^{\prime}},\delta}|\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{Z}_{n}\setminus I^{\prime}}]\big|\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{Z}_{n}\setminus I}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{P}[{X}_{[1,i_{o}]}\in A_{r-X_{I\setminus I^{\prime}},\delta^{\prime}}|\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{Z}_{n}\setminus I^{\prime}}]\big|\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{Z}_{n}\setminus I}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\mathbb{E}\left[\kappa_{I^{\prime}}(\delta^{\prime})\big|\mathscr{X}_{\mathbb{Z}_{n}\setminus I}\right]=\kappa_{I^{\prime}}(\delta^{\prime}).

∎Thus, for any
\delta>0
,
\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)\leq\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\kappa_{[j,j+i_{o}+1]}
where
i_{o}=\lfloor\frac{i}{2}\rfloor
. Thus, we obtain the following averaged version of
Lemma

4.5
.

Lemma A.6
.

Suppose that Assumptions (
MIN-VAR
) and (
MIN-EV
) hold.
For any
i\in[6,n)
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{i_{o}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\left(\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\varepsilon}~(\nu_{1,j+1}+\nu_{1,j+i_{o}})~\kappa_{j+(1,i_{o})}(\varepsilon^{o})+\mu_{j+(1,i_{o})}\right)

\displaystyle+\min\left\{1,C\frac{\delta+2\varepsilon^{o}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{i_{o}-2}}\right\}+\frac{C}{\sigma_{\min}}\overline{\nu}_{1,(1,i)}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{i_{o}-2}},

where
i_{o}\equiv\lfloor\frac{i}{2}\rfloor
and
\varepsilon^{o}\equiv 20\varepsilon\sqrt{\log(p(n_{o}-2))}
, as long as
\varepsilon\geq\sigma_{\min}

Dual Induction.

Let our induction hypothesis on
n
be

\sqrt{n}\mu_{[1,n]}\leq\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}\overline{L}_{3}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}\overline{\nu}_{q}^{1/(q-2)},

(HYP-BE-3)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{1}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{2}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2/(q-2)}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

for universal constants
\mathfrak{C}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{2}
whose values do not change in this subsection.
If
\mathfrak{C}_{1},\mathfrak{C}_{2},\geq 1
, then (
HYP-BE-3
), requiring
\mu_{[1,n]}\leq 2
only, trivially holds for
n\leq 16
.

Now we consider the case of
n>16
. Suppose that the induction hypotheses hold for all intervals with lengths smaller than
n
. We first derive an anti-concentration inequality for any such intervals. Without loss of generality, we only consider the intervals
[1,i]
with
i<n
. We claim that

\sqrt{i}\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)\leq\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i}\overline{L}_{3,[1,i]}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i}\overline{\nu}_{q,[1,i]}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i}\overline{\nu}_{2,[1,i]}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}\delta,

(HYP-AC-3)

where
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{1,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{2,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pi)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
and

\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}=\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
for some universal constants
\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}
, and
\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}
whose values do not change over lines. If
\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa},\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa},\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa},\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa},\geq 1
, then (
HYP-BE-3
), requiring
\kappa_{[1,n]}(\delta)\leq 1
for all
\delta>0
only, trivially holds for
i\leq 16
. For
i>16
, assume that (
HYP-AC-3
) holds for all smaller
i
’s.
By
Lemma

A.6
,
for any
\varepsilon\geq\sigma_{\min}
and
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{i_{o}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\varepsilon}(\nu_{1,j+1}+\nu_{1,j+i_{o}})\min\{1,\kappa_{j+(1,i_{o})}(\varepsilon^{o})\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{i_{o}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\mu_{j+(1,i_{o})}+C\frac{\delta+2\varepsilon^{o}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{i_{o}-2}}+C\frac{\overline{\nu}_{1,(1,i)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\frac{\log(ep)}{\sqrt{i_{o}-2}},

where
i_{o}=\lfloor\frac{i}{2}\rfloor
,
\varepsilon^{o}=20\varepsilon\sqrt{\log(p(i_{o}-2))}
and
C>0
is an absolute constant.
Due to (
HYP-AC-3
) and (
HYP-BE-3
) on intervals
j+(1,i_{o})\subsetneq[1,i]
,

\displaystyle\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{(i_{o}-1)^{3/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\varepsilon}(\nu_{1,j+1}+\nu_{1,j+i_{o}})

\displaystyle\times\left[\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i_{o}-2}\overline{L}_{3,j+(1,i_{o})}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i_{o}-2}\overline{\nu}_{q,j+(1,i_{o})}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i_{o}-2}\overline{\nu}_{2,j+(1,i_{o})}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4}{\delta}\right]

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(i_{o}-1)^{3/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\widetilde{\mu}_{1,i_{o}-2}{\overline{L}_{3,j+(1,i_{o})}}+\frac{C}{(i_{o}-1)^{3/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\widetilde{\mu}_{2,i_{o}-2}{\overline{L}_{4,j+(1,i_{o})}^{1/2}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{(i_{o}-1)^{3/2}}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\widetilde{\mu}_{3,i_{o}-2}{\overline{\nu}_{q,j+(1,i_{o})}^{1/(q-2)}}+C\frac{\delta+2\varepsilon^{o}}{\sigma_{\min}}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{i_{o}-2}}+C\frac{\overline{\nu}_{1,(1,i)}}{\sigma_{\min}}\frac{\log(ep)}{\sqrt{i_{o}-2}}.

To provide an upper bound in terms of
\overline{L}_{3}
and
\overline{\nu}_{q}
, we use the following lemma based on Jensen’s inequality.

Lemma A.7
.

Suppose that
j,k\geq\frac{n}{2}
. For any
q_{1},q_{2},q_{3}>0
and
\alpha\leq 1
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}L_{q_{1},j_{o}+j}\overline{L}_{q_{2},(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}^{\alpha}\leq C\overline{L}_{q_{1}}\overline{L}_{q_{2}}^{\alpha}

and

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\frac{L_{q_{1},j_{o}+j}}{j-1}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-1}L_{q_{2},j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-1}}\overline{L}_{q_{3},j_{o}+(l,k+l)_{j-1}}^{\alpha}\leq C\overline{L}_{q_{1}}\overline{L}_{q_{2}}\overline{L}_{q_{3}}^{\alpha},

where
j_{o}+(k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-1}
is the shifted interval of
(k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-1}
by
j_{o}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, namely,
\{j_{o}+[k_{o}+1]_{j-1},\dots,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k-1]_{j-1}\}
. The same inequality holds when some
L
is replaced with
\nu
.

Based on the lemma,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{i_{o}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}\overline{L}_{3,j+(1,i_{o})}

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{L}_{3,(2,j-1)},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{i_{o}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}(\nu_{1,j+1}+\nu_{1,j+i_{o}})\overline{L}_{3,j+(1,i_{o})}

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{\nu}_{1,(1,i)}\overline{L}_{3,(2,i-1)},

\displaystyle\frac{1}{i_{o}-1}\sum_{j=1}^{i_{o}-1}(\nu_{1,j+1}+\nu_{1,j+i_{o}})\overline{\nu}_{2,j+(1,i_{o})}^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\overline{\nu}_{1,(1,i)}\overline{\nu}_{2,(2,i-1)}^{1/2}.

Similar inequalities hold with
\overline{\nu}_{q}^{1/(q-2)}
in place of
\overline{L}_{3}
.
As a result, we obtain a similar recursive inequality on
\widetilde{\kappa}
’s with
Eq.

22
except that
L_{3,\max}
,
\nu_{q,\max}
,
\nu_{1,\max}
and
\nu_{2,\max}
are replaced with
\overline{L}_{q,[1,i]}
,
\overline{\nu}_{q,[1,i]}
,
\overline{\nu}_{1,[1,i]}
and
\overline{\nu}_{2,[1,i]}
, respectively. Solving the recursive inequality, we prove (
HYP-AC-3
).
The proof of (
HYP-BE-3
) also proceeds similarly using
Lemma

A.7
.
In the end, we obtain a similar recursive inequality for
\widetilde{\mu}
’s with
Eq.

24
except that
L_{3,\max}
and
\nu_{q,\max}
are replaced with
\overline{L}_{3}
and
\overline{\nu}_{q}
, respectively. Solving the recursive inequality, we prove the desired (
HYP-BE-3
).

A.4
Proof of Theorem
2.2

We recall from
Section

A.3
that

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})\right\rvert+\left\lvert\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n)})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})+\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\left\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y}^{(3)}\right\rvert+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2)}\right]\right\rvert.

In
Section

4.4
, we improved the rate by decomposing the third order remainder
\mathfrak{R}^{(3,1)}_{W_{j}}
when the fourth moments were finite. Namely,

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,1)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=3}^{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert.

In
1
-ring dependence cases, there exists additional third-order remainder terms
\mathfrak{R}^{(3)}_{W}
and
\mathfrak{R}^{(3,2)}_{X_{j},W_{k}}
, which came up while breaking the
1
-ring dependence in
Section

A.3
. Based on Tyalor expansions up to order
4
and the second moment matching between
X_{j}
and
Y_{j}
, we decompose the additional remainder terms. First,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)}

(36)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{6}\left\langle\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-1)}),X_{n}^{\otimes 3}\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-3)}),X_{n-2}\otimes X_{n-1}\otimes X_{n}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\left\langle\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(2,n-2)}),X_{n-1}\otimes X_{n}\otimes X_{1}\right\rangle+\left\langle\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(3,n-1)}),X_{n}\otimes X_{1}\otimes X_{2}\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(1,n-2)}),X_{n-1}\otimes X_{n}\otimes(X_{n-1}+X_{n})\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(2,n-1)}),X_{n}\otimes X_{1}\otimes(X_{1}+X_{n})\right\rangle

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(4)},

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(4)}
is specified in
Section

C
. This is the same for
\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n)})
but with
Y
in place of
X
.
To bound the third-order moment terms, we re-apply the Lindeberg swapping and the Taylor expansion up to the sixth order as we did to
\left\langle\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\varepsilon}(X_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+1,n)})],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{\otimes 3}]\right\rangle
in
Section

4.4
. As a result,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(3)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y}^{(3)}\right\rvert\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\left\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(4)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y}^{(4)}\right\rvert+\sum_{k=2}^{n-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X,X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X,Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert,

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X,W_{k}}^{(6)}
is the sixth-order remainder term specified in
Section

C
.

For
\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2)}\right]\right\rvert
, by the Taylor expansion up to the fourth order,

\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(3,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(3,2)}\right]=\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,2)}\right],

where
\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,2)}
is the fourth-order remainder, specified in
Section

C.3
.
Putting all the above results together, we get

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\left\lvert\mathfrak{R}_{X}^{(4)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y}^{(4)}\right\rvert+\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,1)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j}}^{(4,2)}-\mathfrak{R}_{Y_{j}}^{(4,2)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=2}^{n-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X,X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X,Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert+\sum_{j=3}^{n-1}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},X_{k}}^{(6)}-\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},Y_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert.

Remainder lemma.
Similar to
Section

4.1
, the remainder terms are upper bounded by conditional anti-concentration probability bounds.
For
q>0
, let

\widetilde{L}_{q,j}\equiv\sum_{j^{\prime}=j-4}^{j+4}L_{q,j^{\prime}}\enskip\text{and}\enskip\widetilde{L}_{q,[k]_{j-2}}\equiv\sum_{k^{\prime}=k-4}^{k+4}L_{q,[k^{\prime}]_{j-2}},

where
[k^{\prime}]_{j-2}
is
k^{\prime}
modulo
j-2
, and
\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j}
and
\widetilde{\nu}_{q,[k]_{j-2}}
are similarly defined.

Lemma A.8
.

Suppose that Assumption (
MIN-EV
) holds. For
W
representing either
X
or
Y
and
j,k\in\mathbb{Z}_{n}
such that
k\leq j-2
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W}^{(4)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,n}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,n}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{(3,n-2)}(\delta_{0}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{n},1\},

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(4,1)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,j}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{[1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j},1\},

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{W_{j}}^{(4,2)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\left[\begin{aligned} \left(\widetilde{L}_{4,j}+\widetilde{L}_{4,n}\right)\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\left(\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,n}\right)\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{(1,j-2)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j},1\},

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X,W_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\widetilde{L}_{3,n}\left[\begin{aligned} \widetilde{L}_{3,[k]_{n-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,[k]_{n-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{[1,k-3)}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k},1\}.

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\mathfrak{R}_{X_{j},W_{k}}^{(6)}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\widetilde{L}_{3,j}\left[\begin{aligned} \widetilde{L}_{3,[k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,[k]_{j-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\end{aligned}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{[1,k-3)}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k},1\}.

where
\delta_{n-j}^{2}\equiv\delta^{2}+\underline{\sigma}^{2}\max\{n-j,0\}
,
\delta^{o}_{n-j}\equiv 12\delta_{n-j}\sqrt{\log(pn)}
and
\kappa^{o}_{j}\equiv\frac{\delta_{n-j}\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}\sqrt{\max\{j,1\}}}
, as long as
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
and
\phi\delta\geq\frac{1}{\log(ep)}
.

Permutation argument.
Back to
Eq.

31
, for
3\leq j\leq n-1
,

\displaystyle\left\langle\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+1,n]})-\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,j-1)}+Y_{(j+1,n]})],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{\otimes 3}]\right\rangle

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\Big\langle\mathbb{E}[\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-2}}+X_{[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}+Y_{(k_{o}+k,k_{o}+j-1)_{j-2}\cup(j,n]})

\displaystyle-\nabla^{3}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-2}}+Y_{[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}+Y_{(k_{o}+k,k_{o}+j-1)_{j-2}\cup(j,n]})],\mathbb{E}[X_{j}^{\otimes 3}]\Big\rangle

\displaystyle\leq C\phi\frac{\widetilde{L}_{3,j}}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,[k_{o}+k^{\prime}]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,[k_{o}+k^{\prime}]_{j-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{(\delta_{n-k}/2)^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{\kappa_{(k_{o},k_{o}+k-3)_{j-2}}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k},1\}.

The permutation arguement also applies to the first Lindeberg swapping in
Eq.

30
. Together with the results in
Lemma

A.8
,

\displaystyle\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{[1,n]})]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y_{[1,n]})]\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}+X_{j_{o}+j}+Y_{(j_{o}+j,j_{o}+n]})-\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}+Y_{j_{o}+j}+Y_{(j_{o}+j,j_{o}+n]})\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,j_{o}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{(\delta/2)^{q-1}}\right]\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(3,n-2)}(\delta_{0}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{n}\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=2}^{n-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,j_{o}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{(\delta_{n-j}/2)^{q-1}}\right]\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-2)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n-1}\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,j_{o}+j}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{(\delta_{n-j}/2)^{q-1}}\right]\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{j}\}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{n}\frac{\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=1}^{j-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{(\delta_{n-k}/2)^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(k_{o},k_{o}+k-3)_{j-2}}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k}\},

where
j_{o}+(k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-1}
is the shifted interval of
(k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-1}
by
j_{o}
in
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
, namely,
\{j_{o}+[k_{o}+1]_{j-1},\dots,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k-1]_{j-1}\}
.

Partitioning the sum.

Again, we partition the summations at
J_{n}=n(1-\frac{\sigma_{\min}^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\log^{2}(4ep)})
. The calculations of the first three summations are similar to those in
Section

A.3
. Here we only take a look at the last summation where the summation iterates.
For
k<J_{n}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{n}\frac{\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=1}^{(j-2)\wedge\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=3}^{n}\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}\sum_{k=1}^{(j-2)\wedge\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}\left[\overline{L}_{3}\frac{n(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{(j-2)\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{n{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{(j-2)\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=3}^{n}\overline{L}_{3}\left[\overline{L}_{3}\frac{n(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{(j-2)\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}^{2}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{n{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{(j-2)\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\left[\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-3}}\right]\log(en),

where the third inequality comes from
Eq.

16
and the last inequality comes from the fact that
\sum_{j=3}^{n}\frac{n}{(j-2)\delta^{2}_{n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor}}\leq\sum_{j=3}^{n}\frac{n}{(j-2)(n-\lfloor J_{n}\rfloor)\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\leq\frac{C}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}}\log(en)
.
For
k>J_{n}
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\min\{1,\kappa_{j_{o}+(k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-2}}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})+\kappa^{o}_{k}\}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}}{j-2}\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\kappa_{j_{o}+(k_{o},k_{o}+k)_{j-2}}(\delta_{n-k}^{o})

\displaystyle+\frac{C}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[\overline{L}_{3,j_{o}+(0,j)}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\overline{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+(0,j)}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]\kappa^{o}_{k}.

Based on
Lemma

A.7
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-1}\left[\overline{L}_{3,(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\overline{\nu}_{q,(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+2)/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{q+2}}\right]\kappa^{o}_{k}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{n^{3/2}}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}\left[\overline{L}_{3,(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-j}^{2}}+\overline{\nu}_{q,(j_{o},j_{o}+j)}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\overline{L}_{3}\left[\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-j}^{2}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}\delta_{n-j}^{q-1}}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\left[\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}\delta^{q-3}}\right]\log\left(1+\frac{\sqrt{n}\underline{\sigma}}{\delta}\right),

where the first and last inequliaties follow
Eqs.

16
and
23
, respectively.
In sum, we obtain the following induction lemma.

Lemma A.9
.

If Assumptions (
MIN-VAR
), (
MIN-EV
) and (
VAR-EV
) hold, then
for any
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
,

\displaystyle\mu_{[1,n]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\phi\sigma_{\min}}+\frac{C}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\left[\overline{L}_{4}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+1)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}(\delta/2)^{q-4}}\right]\log(en)

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{\sqrt{n}}\overline{L}_{3}\left[\overline{L}_{3}\frac{(\log(ep))^{7/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}}+\overline{\nu}_{q}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q+4)/2}}{\underline{\sigma}^{4}\sigma_{\min}(\delta/2)^{q-3}}\right]\log(en)

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,j_{o}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{(\delta_{n-j}/2)^{q-1}}\right]\kappa_{j_{o}+(3,j-2)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n-1}\left[\widetilde{L}_{4,j_{o}+j}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}}{\delta_{n-j}^{3}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+j}\frac{{(\log(ep))}^{(q-1)/2}}{(\delta_{n-j}/2)^{q-1}}\right]\kappa_{j_{o}+(1,j-4)}(\delta_{n-j}^{o})

\displaystyle+\frac{C\phi}{n}\sum_{j_{o}=1}^{n}\sum_{j=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{n}\frac{\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+j}}{j-2}

\displaystyle\times\sum_{k_{o}=1}^{j-2}\sum_{k=\lceil J_{n}\rceil}^{j-2}\left[\widetilde{L}_{3,j_{o}+[k_{o}k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{5/2}}{\delta_{n-k}^{5}}+\widetilde{\nu}_{q,j_{o}+[k_{o}+k]_{j-2}}\frac{(\log(ep))^{(q+2)/2}}{(\delta_{n-k}/2)^{q+2}}\right]

\displaystyle\times\kappa_{j_{o}+(k_{o},k_{o}+k-3)_{j-2}}(\delta_{n-k}^{o}),

for some absolute constant
C>0
.

Dual Induction.
Based on
Lemmas

A.9
and
A.6
, we proceed the dual induction in the same steps as
Section

A.1
, but using
Lemma

A.7
as in
Section

A.3
. As a result, we prove for any
n
,

\sqrt{n}\mu_{[1,n]}\leq\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}\overline{L}_{3}+\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}\overline{L}_{4}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n}\overline{\nu}_{q}^{1/(q-2)},

(HYP-BE-4)

where

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{1,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{1}\frac{(\log(ep))^{3/2}\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right),

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{2,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{2}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mu}_{3,n}

\displaystyle=\mathfrak{C}_{3}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pn)}}{\underline{\sigma}^{2/(q-2)}\sigma_{\min}}\log\left(en\right)

for universal constants
\mathfrak{C}_{1}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{2}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{3}
whose values do not change over lines, and for any
i<n
,

\sqrt{i}\kappa_{[1,i]}(\delta)\leq\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i}\overline{L}_{3,[1,i]}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i}\overline{L}_{4,[1,i]}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i}\overline{\nu}_{q,[1,i]}^{1/(q-2)}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,i}\overline{\nu}_{2,[1,i]}^{1/2}+\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}\delta,

(HYP-AC-4)

where
\widetilde{\kappa}_{1,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{1,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{2,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{2,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{3,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}\widetilde{\mu}_{3,i}
,

\widetilde{\kappa}_{4,i}=\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}\frac{\log(ep)\sqrt{\log(pi)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
and

\widetilde{\kappa}_{5}=\mathfrak{C}_{5,\kappa}\frac{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}{\sigma_{\min}}
for some universal constants
\mathfrak{C}_{1,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{2,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{3,\kappa}
,
\mathfrak{C}_{4,\kappa}
and
\mathfrak{C}_{5,\kappa}
whose values do not change over lines. This proves the desired Berry–Esseen bound.

B
Proofs of Lemmas

B.1
Proof of Lemma
4.2

The smoothing lemma is the result of the serial application of the following two lemmas.
Lemma

B.2
is a corollary of Theorem 2.1 in
Chernozhukov, Chetverikov and
Koike 2023
. We provide a standalone proof in
Section

B.2
.

Lemma B.1
(Lemma 1,
Kuchibhotla and
Rinaldo 2020
).

Suppose that
X
is a
p
-dimensional random vector, and
Y\sim N(0,\Sigma)
is a
p
-dimensional Gaussian random vector. Then, for any
\delta>0
and a standard Gaussian random vector
Z
,

\mu(X,Y)\leq C\mu(X+\delta Z,Y+\delta Z)+C\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sqrt{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

Lemma B.2
.

Suppose that
X
is a
p
-dimensional random vector, and
Y\sim N(0,\Sigma)
is a
p
-dimensional Gaussian random vector. Then, for any
\phi>0
,

\displaystyle\mu\left(X,Y\right)\leq\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(Y)]\right\rvert+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

For any
\delta>0
,

\mu\left(X,Y\right)\leq\mu\left(X+\delta Z,Y+\delta Z\right)+C\frac{\delta\log(ep)}{\sqrt{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

(37)

Then, for any
\delta>0
and
\phi>0
,

\displaystyle\mu\left(X+\delta Z,Y+\delta Z\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(X+\delta Z)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(Y+\delta Z)]\right\rvert+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y)]\right\rvert+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

In sum,

\mu(X,Y)\leq C\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(Y)]\right\rvert+C\frac{\delta\log(ep)+\sqrt{\log(ep)}/\phi}{\sqrt{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

B.2
Proof of Lemma
B.2

By
Lemma

B.1
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[X\in A_{r}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(X)]=\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(Y)]+\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(Y)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[Y\in A_{r+\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1}}]+\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(Y)]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[Y\in A_{r}]+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}},

and similarly,

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}[Y\in A_{r}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[Y\in A_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1}}]+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[f_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1},\phi}(Y)]+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[f_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1},\phi}(X)]+\mathbb{E}[f_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1},\phi}(Y)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1},\phi}(X)]+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{P}[X\in A_{r}]+\mathbb{E}[f_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1},\phi}(Y)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r-\frac{1}{\phi}\mathbf{1},\phi}(X)]+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

Hence,

\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left\lvert\mathbb{P}[X\in A_{r}]-\mathbb{P}[Y\in A_{r}]\right\rvert\leq\sup_{r\in\mathbb{R}^{p}}\left\lvert\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(X)]-\mathbb{E}[f_{r,\phi}(Y)]\right\rvert+\frac{C}{\phi}\sqrt{\frac{\log(ep)}{\min_{i=1,\dots,p}\Sigma_{ii}}}.

B.3
Proof of Remainder Lemmas

We observe that all remainder terms are in forms of

\displaystyle\frac{1}{(\beta-1)!}\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{J_{1}}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+Y_{J_{2}}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\right]dt,

where
1\leq\beta\leq\alpha
and
J_{1},J_{2}
and
J
are subsets of
\mathbb{Z}_{n}
satisfying
\mathscr{X}_{J_{1}}
,
\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}}
and
\mathscr{W}_{\{j_{1},\dots,j_{\alpha-\beta}\}\cup J}
are mutually independent. We also note that
\mathscr{X}_{J_{1}}\cup\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}}\cup\{W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}\}
is independent from
\mathscr{W}_{\{j_{1},\dots,j_{\alpha-\beta}\}}
. Here we prove a succinct form of the remainder lemmas:

Lemma B.3
.

Suppose that
\operatorname{Var}[Y_{J_{2}}|\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}}]\succeq\underline{\sigma}_{J_{2}}^{2}I_{p}
for some
\underline{\sigma}^{2}_{J_{2}}>0
and
p
-dimensional identity matrix
I_{p}
.
Then, for any
\gamma_{1},\gamma_{2}\in[0,1]
and
\eta>0
, remainder terms in the above form satisfy

\displaystyle\left\lvert\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{J_{1}}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+Y_{J_{2}}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\right]dt\right\rvert

\displaystyle\leq C\left[\frac{(\log(ep))^{\alpha/2}}{{\delta^{\prime}}^{\alpha}}\min\left\{1,\kappa_{J_{1}}\left(\delta^{o}\right)+\kappa^{o}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\times\left[\begin{aligned} &\frac{\phi^{\gamma_{1}}{\delta^{\prime}}^{\gamma_{1}}}{(\log(ep))^{\gamma_{1}/2}}\left\lVert\mathbb{E}\left[\left\lvert(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rvert\right]\right\rVert_{\infty}\\
&+\frac{\phi^{\gamma_{2}}{\delta^{\prime}}^{\gamma_{2}-\eta}}{(\log(ep))^{(\gamma_{2}-\eta)/2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\prod_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}\lVert W_{j_{k}}\rVert_{\infty}\lVert W_{J}\rVert_{\infty}^{\beta}(\lVert W_{J}\rVert_{\infty}^{\eta}+\lVert W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}\rVert_{\infty}^{\eta})\right]\end{aligned}\right],

where
\left\lvert(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rvert
is the element-wise absolute operation,
\delta^{\prime}\equiv\sqrt{\delta^{2}+\underline{\sigma}_{J_{2}}^{2}}
,
\delta^{o}\equiv 12\delta^{\prime}\sqrt{\log(p\lvert J_{1}\rvert)}
, and
\kappa^{o}\equiv\frac{\delta^{\prime}\log(ep)}{\sigma_{\min}\sqrt{\lvert J_{1}\rvert}}
as long as
\delta\geq\sigma_{\min}
and
\phi\delta\geq\frac{1}{\log(ep)}
.

We prove the above generalized remainder lemma here. Because
\operatorname{Var}[Y_{J_{2}}|\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}}]\succeq\underline{\sigma}_{J_{2}}^{2}I_{p}
,

Y_{J_{2}}|\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}}\overset{d}{=}Y^{o}_{J_{2}}+\underline{\sigma}_{J_{2}}\cdot Z,~~\text{almost surely},

where
Y^{o}_{J_{2}}
is the Gaussian random variable with mean
\mathbb{E}[Y_{J_{2}}|\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}}]
and variance
\operatorname{Var}[Y_{J_{2}}|\mathscr{Y}_{J_{2}^{\mathsf{C}}}]-\underline{\sigma}_{J_{2}}^{2}I_{p}
.
For brevity, let
W^{o}\equiv X_{J_{1}}+Y^{o}_{J_{2}}
.
Because
\delta^{\prime}=\sqrt{\delta^{2}+\underline{\sigma}_{J_{2}}^{2}}
, the remainder term is decomposed into

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta}(X_{J_{1}}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+Y_{J_{2}}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\right]dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta^{\prime}}(W^{o}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\right]dt

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta^{\prime}}(W^{o}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\mathbb{I}_{W_{J},1}\right]dt

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha-1}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta^{\prime}}(W^{o}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\mathbb{I}_{W_{J},2}\right]dt

\displaystyle=:\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}T_{W_{J},1}(t)dt+\int_{0}^{1}(1-t)^{\beta-1}T_{W_{J},2}(t)dt,

where

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{W_{J},1}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}_{\{\lVert W_{J}\rVert_{\infty}\text{ and }\lVert W^{{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}}\rVert_{\infty}<\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}\}},

\displaystyle\mathbb{I}_{W_{J},2}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{I}_{\{\lVert W_{J}\rVert_{\infty}\text{ or }\lVert W^{{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}}\rVert_{\infty}\geq\frac{\delta^{\prime}}{\sqrt{\log(ep)}}\}}.

We upper-bound the terms
T_{W_{J},1}(t)
and
T_{W_{J},2}(t)
separately. First,

\displaystyle\left\lvert T_{W_{J},1}(t)\right\rvert

(38)

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\left\langle\nabla^{\alpha}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta^{\prime}}(W^{o}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+tW_{J}),(\otimes_{k=1}^{\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}})\otimes W_{J}^{\otimes\beta}\right\rangle\mathbb{I}_{W_{J},1}\right]\right\rvert

\displaystyle=\left\lvert\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i_{1},\dots,i_{\alpha}}\nabla^{(i_{1},\dots,i_{\alpha})}\rho_{r,\phi}^{\delta^{\prime}}(W^{o}+W^{\not\!\perp\!\!\!\!\perp}+tW_{J})\cdot\prod_{k\leq\alpha-\beta}W_{j_{k}}^{(i_{k})}\prod_{k>\alpha-\beta}W_{J}^{(i_{k})}\cdot\mathbb{I}_{W_{J},1}\right]\right\rvert
